It does this by "broadcasting" the smaller array to the shape of the larger array, so that they can be operated on element-wise.\nLet\'s break down how broadcasting works and explore it with an example:\n### Basic Broadcasting Rules\n1. **Matching trailing dimensions**: If the two arrays differ in their number of dimensions, the shape of the one with fewer dimensions is padded with ones on its leading (left) side.\n2. **Size 1 in either array**: If the shape of the two arrays does not match in any dimension, the array with shape 1 in that dimension is stretched to match the other array These are then broken down into smaller chunks and executed in sync at many levels.\nWhy Parallel Computing?\nParallel computing helps to process information faster. These has numerous applications in the field of machine learning, artificial intelligence, data analysis and simulations.\nGoals of Parallel Computing\nTime reduction: Parallel computing helps in time reduction up to an order of magnitude. This is achieved by dividing the large chunks of work into smaller ones and executing them simultaneously. This reduces the time required to execute the process as a whole.\nFunctional speed-up: This brings uniformity in processing. Each processor works in sync completing their special tasks efficiently. This leads to uniformity in overall output rather than mistakes in sequential tasks.\nScaling up: In traditional computing methods, as data or task size increases, we need to upgrade our hardware. Parallel computing helps in scaling up the efficiency without a significant increase in hardware.\nApplications of Parallel Computing\nVideo and audio processing: Parallel computing helps in the smooth functioning of video and audio editors. The software can now run multiple tasks simultaneously without crashes or reduction in performance.\nCryptography: It’s an important part of the secure world of the internet. It is a powerful tool for efficiently performing element-wise operations between arrays.\n\n## Basic Principle\nThe basic principle of broadcasting is to align arrays with different shapes by adding dimensions (axes) of size one, allowing them to match in their shapes. Once aligned, operations can be performed element-wise.\n\n## Rules of Broadcasting\nThe following are the rules of broadcasting in NumPy:\n\n1. **Matching trailing axes**: If the two arrays differ in their number of dimensions, the shape of the one with fewer dimensions is padded with ones on its leading (left) side.\n2. **Dimension sizes**: When the sizes of the arrays do not match in any dimension, the size of one of the arrays in that dimension must be one. It serves as a way to organize and manage resources, such as web apps, databases, storage, and networking components, that are required to support an application. Resource groups provide a scope for applying role-based access control (RBAC), tags, and policies. You can consider a resource group as a folder that contains all the resources needed to run your application.\n\n### App Service Plans\n\nAn **App Service Plan** (also known as a plan) defines the resources and scaling settings for your web application. Plans are essentially the pricing tier and compute resources allocated to your web application.\n\nThink of an App Service Plan as the "lease" for your web application\'s infrastructure. By dividing a problem into smaller sub-problems and solving them concurrently, parallel computing enables faster processing of large datasets, simulations, and complex algorithms.\nGoals of Parallel Computing:\n1. Speedup: To achieve faster execution times for complex computations.\n2. Scalability: To enable the solution of larger problems by adding more processing resources.\n3. Efficiency: To reduce the time and energy required for computations.\nApplications of Parallel Computing:\n1. Scientific Simulations: Climate modeling, fluid dynamics, and materials science.\n2.
